- **N6c, N6b's "stays at" when Apply moves a set.** A set the answer carries on at another weight than it has now reads "set 2 had no weight logged, carries on at 15 kg" (and "carries on at 1,999 kg, as a step would pass 2,000 kg"); "stays at" is kept for a set already at that weight. The other way: N6b's "stays at" whatever the set holds. Recommendation: keep, since Apply would move it.
- **N6c, another space's `X-Tenant-Id`.** The row's tests asked 404 for an `X-Tenant-Id` the caller is not a member of; the API answers 403 there, as on every route (the tenant handler), and 404 for another space's client asked from one's own space. Built and tested as the API answers. Recommendation: keep; the plan's word was a slip.
- **N6c, outside the row: README's "Five ways to set a load".** N6a's line said to say six once N6c lands; README is not this row's, so it still says five. Recommendation: "Six ways to set a load: …, or last time plus a step the coach applies", in a docs commit of its own.
- **N6d, a kept weight counts.** A hold that keeps last time's weight on a set at another weight (N6c's "Keep 25 kg" with Apply 25 kg) counts as a weight to apply, so the line opens a panel that lists exactly what it counted, and the row's "gone once ... held" means a hold that keeps each set's own weight (a likely typo, another unit, a workout still running). The other way: step-ups alone, so a hold on a lighter set (next week's set at its program weight, or a new program's starting weight) never reaches Today and the client trains at that weight unless the coach opens the tab. Recommendation: keep.
- **N6d, the words.** The row's "<name>: 3 lifts ready to step up" in the sentence every Today row uses: "Maya Fakhoury has 3 lifts ready to step up" ("has a lift ready to step up"), and "has 2 weights to apply" or "has a weight to apply" when one of them keeps last time's weight; the Clients tag and a client's page say "3 lifts ready to step up" or "2 weights to apply" (the tag reads "Maya Fakhoury: 3 lifts ready to step up" to a screen reader); under it "Next workout today", "tomorrow" or "Tue 17 Nov", the soonest of those workouts; the Weights tag in the share tone (the green of Week not shared and of the old maxes item); and Review weights, which opens the panel and applies nothing. The other way: "Steps to review · 3", or "weights to apply" always. Recommendation: keep.
- **N6d, one per writing.** A lift written twice in one session (a top set and a back-off) counts twice, as the panel lists it and "Apply all 2 weights" counts it. The other way: distinct lifts. Recommendation: keep; Today's number is the panel's.
- **N6d, never Needs attention.** The row's default: ranked last, after No program; a client with this reason alone keeps the plan's own status on Clients, with the tag beside the pill, and the greeting counts them, as it counts plans ending. The other way: Needs attention. Recommendation: keep.
- **N6d, where it shows.** Clients carries it as a tag (the row's default), and a client's Overview and the chat's client panel as a row with Review weights, since both draw the server's items and a client's page never disagrees with the list (CLI-018). The other way: Today and Clients only. Recommendation: keep.
- **N6d, the gate.** Counted while training is in today's plan, as every training signal is (CLI-018); the Training tab opens a little wider (TRN-023: before a booked plan begins), so in that gap the panel offers a weight Today does not name. The other way: TRN-023's gate. Recommendation: keep; a client whose plan has not begun cannot train yet.
- **N6d, the order and the day.** Within the kind, the client whose soonest workout with a weight is nearest comes first, then by last name; the day is the program's own, so a program left in an older zone after the workspace moved reads "Next workout today" on a day the workspace has already passed. The other way: most lifts first. Recommendation: keep.
- **N6d, the arrival.** Review weights brings Weights to apply to the middle of the screen at once (no smooth scroll), with the focus on its heading; when nothing is left (applied in another tab, the workout started meanwhile) the page says "Nothing is left to apply for Maya now." and the focus goes to the program's title; a closed training read says only what the tab already says. The other way: silence, as an old `?session=` is, or the lift's session sheet opened at its Apply. Recommendation: keep.
- **N6d, the statuses sheet's words.** "Lifts ready to step up", after No program yet: "A lift set to Last time + step can go up by its step: in their last workout of it, within 14 days, every working set reached its reps and none felt harder than planned. A lift that held for its reps or its effort shows as a weight to apply when its next workout is set at another weight than the one it keeps; a likely typo, another unit or a workout still running shows nothing. It clears once you apply it on their Training, once that workout starts, or once the lift holds at its own weight. It doesn't count as needing attention." The other way: both tag forms as its title. Recommendation: keep until the trainer partner reads it.
- **N6d, Today's read of a lift left without a weight.** A cost to know: Today, the list and a client's page read each lift's last 15 days, and the workouts before them only for a lift whose last 15 days hold no log near a weight its workout asked (a step set with no starting weight before the coach first applies, or left unapplied for two weeks), since a first log that was a typo can hold such a lift for good (N6b's guard); for such a lift the read grows with its history, on each refresh of Today (every 4 s while the coach is there). The other way: keep the guard's standing on each finished set, or let the guard forget after some weeks (a LastTimeStep v2, your rule), either of which ends it. Recommendation: the stored standing, as a small row of its own, before coaches use step sets without a starting weight at scale.
- **N6d, outside the row: README.** README's Today line does not name weights to apply, and its "Five ways to set a load" still says five (N6a's and N6c's lines). Recommendation: both in one docs commit.
- **N6d, outside the row: the demo and the homepage picture.** The demo's three leg curls each keep 25 kg over a set with no weight, so Lea's and Omar's Today end with "has a weight to apply" lines (Karim's with all three), and the homepage's `today-needs-you` picture, taken from the demo, would show them when taken again. Recommendation: keep; take it again with the hero's morning retake.
- **N7, a 100% discount's words.** "free until <last day>", in lower case inside the sentence, and "free from <first day> until <last day>" for one still to come: "Early coach offer: free until Dec 15, 2026, then 35% off until Mar 15, 2027, then 12.5% off until Jun 15, 2027, then the normal price." The other way: "100% off until <last day>". Recommendation: keep.
- **N7, stretches in a row at one rate.** Two free stretches in a row (decision 13's free months, then the free start) read as one clause until the later last day, the row's default; the build does the same for any two in a row at one rate, which the row did not name: a 30% then a 30% read "30% off until Dec 31, 2026", not "30% off until Nov 30, 2026, then 30% off until Dec 31, 2026". The other way: free stretches alone as one and other equal rates a clause each (one condition in `discountSentences` and one case in its spec), or a clause each always. Recommendation: keep; lengthening a discount means granting another at the same rate.
- **N7, a run with different names.** One sentence, named after the run's first discount still running or to come, so a later stretch's note is not shown on the owner's page, though the grant sheet says the owner sees the note as the name (the admin's page still lists each with its note); once a stretch ends it leaves the sentence, so the day after the free start's last day the sentence opens with the next rate and that stretch's note. The other way: a new sentence at each new name. Recommendation: keep, and type one note on every stretch of one offer.
- **N7, the name is the note.** The owner reads the admin's "What it is for" note as the discount's name, and the grant sheet says under the field "The owner sees this as the discount’s name."; a note typed before N7 was typed for the admin alone and now shows to the space's owner (no space holds a real discount before go-live). The other way: a "Name" field beside "What it is for". Recommendation: keep.
- **N7, the line's place and form.** Each sentence is a paragraph of its own right after the status sentence in the hero, so it follows the trial sentence and an overdue or read-only space's sentence too; the name keeps its own direction, so one typed in Arabic keeps the line's order; the days take the page's own form, "Dec 15, 2026" (with the year, as every billing day there), not the words rule's "Tue 15 Dec". The other way: "Tue 15 Dec" days, or the line inside This month so far. Recommendation: keep; a run can cross a year.
- **N7, how every sentence ends.** A run still to come reads "Summer return: 10% off from Jul 1, 2027 until Aug 31, 2027, then the normal price.", ending with the normal price like a running one, since that is what follows its last day; two runs with days between them are two sentences that both end so. The other way: no "then the normal price" when another run follows later. Recommendation: keep.
- **N7, outside the row: "1 seats" during the trial.** This month so far reads "Free trial: nothing to pay this month so far. You have 1 seats and 0 clients with a coaching plan." for a space with one seat (`billing.html`, since b4d81cf). Recommendation: "1 seat" for one, in a words fix of its own.
- **N11a, several seats.** A founding space with more than one seat (a gym) has the founding number on its owner's seat alone, and every other seat includes the plan's number, the row's default (Open 1): with the plan's 5 a seat and a founding number of 12, four seats include 27. The other way: every seat of a founding space takes the founding number. Recommendation: keep while every founding space is one coach; decide before a founding gym adds coaches.
- **N11a, a founding number at or below the plan's.** The larger of the two applies, so a founding seat never includes fewer clients than a plain one (Open 2); the invoice still records the number, its version and the status, and the owner's read and the admin's founding view name no number then. The other way: the founding number always, even below the plan's. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11a, two grants in one month.** They add up (Open 4): a coach who brought two coaches has both grants' clients that month. The other way: the larger grant alone, as with discounts. Recommendation: keep; decision 17 gives clients for each coach brought.
- **N11a, a lower number later.** Each founding space keeps the highest number it has had while its status stays open (Open 5): the highest among the terms versions in force at some moment from UTC midnight of its founding day to its end, up to the moment the bill is issued; a higher number applies from the next invoice, and a version that lowers the number or names none never takes clients from an open status. Two costs to know: a number published by mistake stays with every founding space that was open while it was in force (only an end and a new mark start a status afresh), and a founding day set in the past reaches the versions in force on that day. The other way: the lower number applies from the next invoice to every founding space, as a new plan version does. Recommendation: keep, and publish a number with care.
- **N11a, a grant on a space that is not founding.** Refused with 409 `space_not_founding`, on a space never marked and on one whose status ended (Open 6); a grant already made stays when the status later ends, until the admin revokes it. The other way: a grant on any space, as a discount is. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11a, a grant's days.** Whole UTC calendar months (Open 7): its first day and the day after its last are each the 1st of a month, from January 2000, so a day left out of a hand call (read as year 1) is refused; any other day is refused by field, and the database checks the same. The other way: any days, a grant off a month's edge adding to both months it touches. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11a, a reissue's founding terms.** A void and reissue keeps the voided invoice's plan and counts the founding seat again as it stands now (Open 8): the highest number the status had up to the reissue, so a number published after the month but while the status ran reaches the corrected month, and a revoked grant leaves it; a version published after the status ended counts for none of its months, though Open 8's "with the terms in force now" could be read to take it. The other way: keep the voided invoice's founding terms version, as its plan is kept. Recommendation: keep; it is how a late mark, number or grant is put right.
- **N11a, the owner's grants.** The owner's read carries each running or coming grant's clients, first day, last day, the day after it and whether it starts later, the shape of N7's discounts, so N11b adds nothing up; the row named only the first day and the day after the last. Never the note. The other way: the two days alone. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11a, technical choices (Open 9).** A publish says none by sending the key as null; the key is required by the JSON reader, so a publish that leaves it out gets 400 `request_invalid` before any check and publishes nothing, naming the key: "This request is missing foundingSeatIncludedClients. Reload the page and send it again." (the unreadable-request handler now names any such key, and a body it cannot read at its root no longer names the code's parameter `request`), while a number out of range gets the field's own error. An invoice holds its grants' ids in a `uuid[]` frozen with the row (no foreign key can prove each id, but the server writes them from the space's own grants, which are never deleted), and names its founding status by a composite key in its own space. A grant takes a lock on the space's open founding status, so a double press finds the first grant and a grant never lands on a status that ended meanwhile; a partial unique index stays behind it. The migration refuses to revert once a grant, a published number or an invoice that counted them exists (ADR 0040). The other way: a "none" flag of its own, a child row per grant. Recommendation: keep; N11b's sheet always sends the key.
- **N11a, outside the row: one failing space stops the month's run.** `PlatformInvoiceRun.IssueForMonthAsync` bills the spaces one by one and stops at the first that throws (anything but a duplicate invoice), so every later space waits, on every sweep, until that one is fixed, though the class's summary says a failure "never stops the others". Not changed here. Recommendation: catch, log and count a failed space and go on, in a small row of its own with a test.
- **N11b, the grants panel's place.** "Extra included clients" is a panel of its own right after Discounts, on a founding space or one that still has grants, its rows built like the discounts' (the clients, the days, the note and a pill: Running now, Starts later, Ended, Revoked); a space never founding and never granted shows no panel, so its page is pixel for pixel as before (the row's default). The other way: a second list inside Discounts. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11b, the sheet's default and its days.** Next month alone, its 1st to its last day, so one grant is one invoice (the row's default); "One month", "2 months" and "3 months" count whole calendar months from the first day's month, and a new first day carries the grant's whole months with it (one month when its days were off a month's edge), so moving the first day earlier never stretches a grant unseen. The other way: the month in progress; or a last day that stays where it was when the first day moves. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11b, the owner's words.** One sentence for the founding seat, "As a founding coaching space, your own seat includes 13 clients.", and one per grant, "4 more clients included until Oct 31, 2026." or, for one still to come, "7 more clients included from Dec 1, 2026 until Feb 28, 2027.", each a paragraph after N7's discount line in the hero, during the trial too; one client reads "1 client" (the row's default). The other way: one combined sentence. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11b, the terms panel's heading and the sheet's title.** The list's panel is "Founding terms", holding the badge's line and the founding seat's (the row's default); the sheet it opens and its toast still say "Change the badge’s terms" and "Badge terms version N is published…", though the sheet now holds the seat's number too. The other way: keep "Founding coach badge" and add a second panel. Recommendation: keep the panel, and rename the sheet's title and toast to "Change the founding terms" and "Founding terms version N…" in a words pass.
- **N11b, the bill line's breakdown.** Only when a founding seat or a grant counted (the row's default): "Clients with a plan: 14, 30 included: 15 with your seats, 8 more as a founding space, 7 more granted", the "<x> more × <price>" tail after it; a part of 0 is left out, so a plan whose seat includes no clients reads "13 included: 13 more as a founding space", and a plain bill's line is unchanged word for word. The other way: always. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11b, "no founding number is published yet".** The Founding panel, the mark sheet and the list say "…what the price plan’s seat includes: no founding number above it is published yet.", since the server names no number also when the published one is at or below the plan's seat (N11a's Open 2) and the space page cannot tell the two apart; the mark sheet reads the price plan in force beside the terms to word this, and names the number as published when the plan cannot be read. The other way: the row's "no founding number is published yet", true only when none is. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11b, a backdated mark after a lower number.** The mark sheet's "…includes 13 clients (the terms in force)" and the list's "A founding space’s owner seat includes 13 clients." name the terms in force, while a space marked from a day before a version that lowered the number keeps the higher one (N11a's Open 5); once marked, its Founding panel says what the server applies. The other way: "at least 13 clients", always true, or a sentence that weighs the founding day. Recommendation: keep while no version lowers the number; say "at least" if one ever does.
- **N11b, the admin's invoice rows.** The admin's page draws no bill lines under an invoice (its `invoiceLines` helper was never drawn), so an invoice that counted a founding seat or a grant gains one note under its row, the owner's clients line, and its usage line names "founding terms v2"; a plain invoice shows as before. The other way: every invoice's lines on the admin's page. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11b, the founding seat's box.** Its help reads "The price plan’s seat includes 5 clients; the larger applies. Leave it empty for none; a space already founding keeps the highest number it has had." (the row named the first sentence; the other two say what an empty box publishes, and N11a's Open 5); an empty box publishes none and reads "None" in What changes, and a box the browser cannot read keeps what was typed under its error and is never sent as none. The other way: the row's sentence alone. Recommendation: keep.
- **N11b, an ended grant's Revoke.** Revoke shows on a running or coming grant alone, as the row says and as discounts do; but a grant for a past month, made to put that month's invoice right, is Ended the moment it is made, so a mistyped one can be revoked only through the API before that month is issued or reissued (the server revokes any grant not yet revoked). The other way: Revoke on every grant and discount not yet revoked, whose dialog already says that invoices already issued keep it. Recommendation: the other way, for grants and discounts alike, in a small row of its own with a test.
- **N11b, the words the row did not name.** The grant sheet's helps "The 1st of a month." and "A month’s last day. The grant covers this day too." and its refusals in the server's words ("Start on the first day of a month: extra clients are granted for whole months."); the revoke dialog's title "Revoke these extra clients?" and, for one client, "Revoke this extra client?" with "Revoke client" and "Keep it"; the toasts "Extra clients granted." and "Extra clients revoked."; and the mark sheet's line when the terms cannot be read, "…its owner’s seat includes what the terms in force give a founding seat." Recommendation: keep.
- **N11b, outside the row: focus after a discount's Revoke.** Once a grant is revoked its Revoke goes and the panel's heading takes the focus; a discount's Revoke (since R5.2b) leaves the focus on the page when its button goes. Not changed here. Recommendation: the same hand-off for discounts, a few lines with a test.
- **N2, the page's frame.** Signed in, `/about` fills the window as a visitor sees the homepage, in TB Gym's own colours even where a coach has their own, with no sidebar, top bar or client tabs around it, and an app that starts there draws no shell's frame on the way in. The other way: inside the coach frame, as What changed is drawn. Recommendation: keep; it is the page a coach shows someone.
- **N2, where "Back to Today" sits.** Where a visitor sees Sign in: the header on a wide screen, the header's Menu on a phone, and the page's foot. On a phone it is one tap behind Menu, as Sign in is. The other way: a bar across the top of the page. Recommendation: keep, and say so if the installed app on your phone makes the way back hard to find.
- **N2, the group in the menu.** "TB Gym" heads its two rows after Dark mode, in the heading style of "Coaching spaces", with a hairline above and below so that Sign out does not read as one of TB Gym's pages. The other way: first in the menu, or no hairlines. Recommendation: keep.
- **N2, what is left where sign-up is hidden.** Signed in, the hero and the closing line keep "Talk to us about your gym" alone, the six rows have no link, and the coach's half of Who it's for has no button; on a wide screen its screen is held a button's height lower so the two screens stay in line. The Talk-to-us form stays and can be sent, with its stamp and allowances as for a visitor. The other way: hide the form too, or put another button in the coach's half. Recommendation: keep.
- **N2, the clients strip's "Sign in".** The row did not name it. Signed in, the strip reads "Here as a client? Your coach sends the invitation." without "Already joined? Sign in.", since that link would open Today under another name during a pitch. The other way: leave the sentence whole. Recommendation: keep.
- **N2, who can open `/about`.** Anyone: a client or a former client who types it sees the page with Back to Today and no tabs or member bar, and a visitor sees the homepage with Sign in and Start, its links on `/about`, never counted and marked noindex. The other way: send a client to Today and a visitor to `/`. Recommendation: keep; nothing leads either of them there.
- **N2, the title.** `/about` keeps the homepage's title, "TB Gym: coach both sides of the week". The other way: "About TB Gym". Recommendation: keep.
- **N2, the sign-up page's count.** To be sent, the count needs the script's name, so the sign-up page now reads the website's settings as What changed does (one anonymous read, a visitor's alone); the count carries the page's path and campaign tags and never the rest of its address. Nothing counts on `/about`, signed in or not. The other way: none that sends the count for someone who lands on the page directly. Recommendation: keep.
- **N2, analytics for someone signed in.** The row says `/about` may load the analytics script and count nothing. As built, someone signed in gets the website's settings there (for the WhatsApp link, once there is a number) and no script at all: it is started for a visitor alone, as What changed already did. The other way: load it for everyone and count nothing. Recommendation: keep; a signed-in page has no use for it.
- **N2, the installed app.** `/about` is inside the manifest's scope, so it opens in the installed window, where Back to Today is the way back; the browser checks prove the button at 390 px, and the installed app itself is yours to try on a phone (the plan's own step for Point 5). Not verified here.
- **N2, outside the row: focus after "Stay".** The two new rows close the menu and hand focus back to the account button, so after "Stay" on a page with typed work the keyboard is where it was. The menu's older rows (Password and security, the platform admin's three) still leave focus on the page's body after "Stay", as before. Not changed here. Recommendation: the same hand-off for them, a few lines with a test.
- **N2, outside the row: What changed in dark mode.** The menu's second row opens What changed inside the coach frame, and that page is still drawn light whatever the person's mode: with Dark mode on it is a cream page between a dark sidebar and a dark top bar (the still `changelog-owner-1440-dark.png`). It was so before; until this row nothing in the app led a coach there. Not changed here, since the same page is the public one the build renders ahead of time. Recommendation: a small row that marks it rebuilt, lets the copy inside the app take the app's ink in light and dark, and keeps the public page as it is.
- **N2, outside the row: What changed is a month old.** The page the new row opens, and the date `/about` and the homepage print ("Last change 13 Oct 2026"), come from `src/web/src/content/changelog.md`, whose newest day is 13 Oct 2026: nothing built since is on it (Dark mode, the critique and motion rows, this plan's rows, the TB Gym group itself), because no row was told to add its line. Not changed here: they are public words, and a month of them is more than this row. The other way: add this row's line alone, which would print today's date over a list that misses a month. Recommendation: one row that writes the missing days in a coach's words for you to read, and a line in every later row's checks to add its own.
- **N8, whose months count.** The months are read from the founding terms in force when a bill is worked out, for every founding space, the ones already founding too (the row's "the terms in force"; the box's help says so): a version with more months lengthens every guarantee, and one with fewer months or none shortens or ends them from the next invoice. The other way: each founding space keeps the most months its status has had, as its seat keeps the highest number (N11a). Recommendation: keep while every founding coach is promised the same months; if a later wave is promised fewer, say so before publishing them, and a small row makes each space keep its own.
- **N8, the version of the founding day.** A founding space is held at the last version published by the end of its founding UTC day, or by the moment it was marked when that comes first: prices published in the morning and a space marked that afternoon go together, a founding day typed in the past reaches the version in force that day (the first version for a day before any publish), and no version published after the mark, not even later that day, moves a space whose pages already say its prices are held. So a space marked before the real prices are published is held at the placeholder ones and pays whichever is lower; ending the status and marking it again puts that right. The other way: the end of the founding day whatever the mark, so a plan published the evening of the mark would be the one held. Recommendation: keep, and publish the real prices before the first founding day you type.
- **N8, the day the pages name.** "Your prices are held until <day>." and "Prices held at plan version <n> until <day>." name the guarantee's last day itself, as a discount's sentence names its last day, though the whole month that day falls in is billed at the held version (answer d). Both lines go the day after it, and at once when the status ends, while that month's bill is still held. The other way: name the last day of that month. Recommendation: keep; the day named is the one promised, and the bill is never worse than it says.
- **N8, a discount granted late.** The months count from the last unrevoked discount whenever it was granted, so a discount granted after a guarantee has ended starts it again (the founding day's version, until the months after that discount), and revoking the last discount moves the last day back, into the past if it comes to that. The other way: only a discount granted before the guarantee ended counts. Recommendation: keep; it is the row's rule, and the admin's two steps for ending a founding price early depend on it. Know it when giving a founding space a later discount.
- **N8, "whichever is lower".** The held version is compared with the latest version alone, on the bill's total after the discount; equal totals keep the held one, and a version published in between that would have been cheaper than both is never used. The invoice takes everything from the version billed, its days to pay and its grace days too. The other way: the cheapest of every version since the founding day. Recommendation: keep.
- **N8, what an invoice records.** The version billed with its prices, as before; not that the price was held, the rule's name (`founding-price-v1`) or the guarantee's last day. Why a past invoice has its version is read from the plans' publish days, the space's founding status, its discounts and the terms, and two of those can change afterwards (a discount revoked later, new months). Left out as work outside the row, which names no new invoice column. The other way: three columns on the invoice, frozen with it. Recommendation: a small row before the first price change, so every held bill explains itself.
- **N8, the months' range.** 0 to 120, or none. None holds nothing; 0 holds the founding day's version through the month of the last discount day and no month after (with no discount, the founding month alone). The other way: 1 to 24, as the badge's months. Recommendation: keep.
- **N8, ended and marked again in one month.** The status marked last holds that month, so a wrongly dated status is put right by ending it and marking again; but a space ended on 12 July and marked again from 20 July, after a dearer version came out between the two founding days, is billed July at the new status's version ($21 in the test) and not at the ended one's ($15) that answer (c) alone would give, and a reissue keeps it. Marking again from the first founding day keeps the first version. The other way: the lowest total among every status touching the month and the latest version. Recommendation: keep; say so if an end and a new mark in one month should never cost the coach.
- **N8, the month a trial ends.** A bill counts from the trial's end, and it is held when a hold touches what it counts, as a discount applies: a guarantee's last day or a status's end that falls earlier in that same month than the trial's end does not reach that first bill, which is then at the latest version, though answers (c) and (d) read by the calendar would hold it. It needs a founding status that ended, or a guarantee that ran out, before the space's trial did. The other way: the calendar month whatever the bill counts. Recommendation: keep; it is the row's "the way a discount covers every month it touches", and N11a's founding seat ends the same way.
- **N8, what the owner's seat is read against.** "As a founding coaching space, your own seat includes …" (N11b) and the admin's seat line on a space's page were read against the latest plan's seat; they are now read against the version the month is priced at, the held one for a held price, so the sentence and the bill agree when two versions' seats differ. Not changed: the list's seat line, the terms sheet's help and the mark sheet's seat sentence (N11b's) still speak of the latest plan's seat, so with a founding number between an old and a new plan's seats they can differ from a held space's own page. The other way: none that keeps the page and the bill agreeing. Recommendation: keep, and reword those three sentences to say "the latest price plan’s seat" in the row that first publishes a plan with another seat number.
- **N8, the words the row did not name.** The list's line "A founding space keeps its plan version until 5 months after its last discount ends, or after its founding day when it has none." ("…until its last discount ends; with no discount, only for its founding month." for 0, and "A founding space’s prices are not held: no guarantee months are published yet." for none); the sheet's box "Months a founding space’s prices are held" with its help and the refusal "Enter a whole number of months from 0 to 120, or none to hold no price."; the mark sheet's sentence with the months in force; the end sheet's "…and held prices end: the latest price plan applies."; and the price plan sheet and its toast, which say "Founding spaces keep their version until their guarantee ends." only while months are published, and otherwise warn "No guarantee months are published yet, so founding spaces are billed at the new version too.", since the row's sentence would be untrue then. Recommendation: keep; reword freely.
- **N8, technical choices.** The months' key is required on a publish, as the seat's number is: a call that leaves it out gets 400 and publishes nothing, so an old page cannot end every guarantee. The column is nullable with a check and no seed, and the migration refuses to revert once months are published; a revert refused at an earlier migration leaves this one taken back until the database is rolled forward again. A month still inside the trial names the held version on the admin's page. No route was added. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a1, when a trainee becomes a client.** A new person's sign-up stores the account, the documents they accepted and one sign-up row; the membership and the client record are created when the email is confirmed, in that same step, not at sign-up as the outline said. So the house's Owner and Coach never see somebody whose email was not confirmed (the name and email could be typed by anyone), nothing can be booked or written for such a person, and a sign-up never confirmed can be removed cleanly: the coach history, the profile's change history and the acceptances are never deleted, so a client record made at sign-up could not have been. Someone already signed in has a confirmed account and joins at once. The other way: create the record at sign-up and loosen those append-only guards for a sign-up never confirmed. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a1, when a place of the week is taken.** At the confirmation too, not when the form is sent: the week's number counts confirmed sign-ups, so a stranger typing made-up addresses cannot keep sign-up "full for this week" (the review's finding; the first build counted every form). A full week takes no new form. Someone who sent the form while a place was left and confirms after the week filled has their email confirmed and waits: the app tells their page so, and they join with the form, signed in, once a week has room (no list is kept beyond their own waiting sign-up, which is dropped after the sweep's days if they never come back). The other way: hold the place from the form, which keeps the count simple and lets anybody fill the week without a mailbox. Recommendation: keep. Someone with throwaway mailboxes can still take a week's places, as with any open sign-up with a number; a challenge on the form would be a row of its own if it ever happens.
- **N9a1, what "removed" means for a sign-up never confirmed.** The sign-up row goes, with the name and date of birth it held, and the account is emptied: no name, an address under a reserved domain no mail reaches, no password, every link spent. The email can then sign up afresh. The emptied account row itself stays, with the acceptances it recorded and the record that a confirmation email was sent, because those are append-only and name it; it identifies nobody. The other way: delete the row too, which needs those guards loosened. Recommendation: keep, and let N9e define anonymising for confirmed accounts on the same lines.
- **N9a1, the sweep's days, and no second link.** 2 days by default (`TraineeSignUps:RemoveUnconfirmedAfterDays`, 1 to 90; the Worker looks every hour). A confirmation link works for one day and nothing in the app sends a second one, for a trainee or for a coach, so someone who misses the link signs up again once the sweep has freed the email, and the refusal they read meanwhile says so ("if that link has stopped working, sign up again in a few days"). No lifetime setting existed "beside the confirmation mail's" for it to sit next to, so it has a section of its own. The other way: a "send the link again" step on the confirmation screen, which would also help a coach whose link ran out (today such a coach's email is stuck: it can neither confirm nor register again). Recommendation: a small row for "send the link again", for coaches and trainees alike; N9a2's confirmation screen can then offer it.
- **N9a1, the age setting.** While the age is empty sign-up is closed, like the week's number: both must be filled to open it. It takes 18 to 100: the platform is adults only (DOMAIN-RULES section 11, and the intake's own check), so the house's age can be higher than 18, never lower. Age is read on the house's day (Beirut), the birthday itself is old enough, and a trainee who later edits their date of birth in the intake cannot move it under the house's age. The other way: an empty age means 18. Recommendation: keep; the age line is the lawyer's, so nothing opens before somebody typed it.
- **N9a1, empty and zero.** Both keep sign-up closed, as the row's default said. Emptying either setting closes sign-up at once, also for someone who sent the form before and confirms after; people already confirmed stay.
- **N9a1, which member of staff a trainee's record names.** Every client record names one member of staff (ADR 0026, until N9d1). A trainee's names the house's Coach who joined its team first, and the Owner while it has no Coach; it is fixed when the email is confirmed. So the trainer partner joins the house's team before sign-up is opened (GO-LIVE says so): the Owner sees every trainee, and a Coach those whose record names them, which is then all of them (decision 33). A trainee confirmed before the Coach joined is moved by the Owner from the client's page, one at a time. The other way: a house setting naming the person, or every Coach of the house seeing every trainee. Recommendation: keep until the house has a second coach; N9d1 revisits who sees whom.
- **N9a1, what "nothing on Coach Today" covers.** A self-directed trainee raises no item in Needs you, no line in the activity feed, none under Plans ending soon or Ended, not renewed, and their row on Clients shows no reason; the count of clients and This month's active clients still include them, and a payment recorded for them still counts as money received. Their workout notes and their leaving reach no inbox, and when a coach leaves the team they are moved to the owner like every client but sent no "your coach has changed" notice or email. A client the house invites in the ordinary way is coached, not self-directed, and raises everything a coach's client does. The other way: leave them out of the counts too. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a1, coming back.** Someone who left the house may sign up again and gets a new record beside the ended one, once in a house week ("You already signed up this week. You can sign up again on Monday."), so nobody takes a week's places or piles up ended records by leaving and returning. Someone whose coaching the house itself ended, or who left while the house had blocked their access, is refused ("This coaching space ended your coaching or blocked your access, so you can't sign up again by yourself. Ask it to invite you back."): a block is not undone by leaving, and the way back is the house's own invitation, which makes them an ordinary coached client. A former member of the house's team is refused too. The other way: let anyone who is not a member today sign up again. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a1, the house's own calendar.** The week the number counts over is the house's calendar week, so its Owner, who can change the space's first day of the week or its time zone in Settings, moves that week by doing so: changing the first day mid-week starts a new count. Only TB Gym's own Owner can do it. The other way: refuse those two changes while sign-up is open, until the platform admin empties a setting. Recommendation: leave; it is the tech partner's own setting.
- **N9a1, the limit on attempts.** Twenty sign-up attempts an hour from one address (every attempt counts, a refused one too), instead of public authentication's thirty a minute plus a second limit: a route carries one limit, and this one is the tighter. A gym's shared connection can sign up several people in an hour. The other way: a looser limit for the trainer partner's gym. Recommendation: keep; watch the House page's "waiting" count when sign-up opens.
- **N9a1, the words the row did not name.** The refusals a form can show: "Sign-up is not open right now."; "Sign-up is full for this week. It opens again on Monday." (the house's own first day of the week); "Sign-up is open to people aged 19 or older." and, when a trainee edits their date of birth later, "This coaching space is open to people aged 19 or older." (the number is the setting; the sentence beside the date-of-birth box is still the lawyer's); "An account already uses this email. Sign in with it, then sign up from there. If you already signed up here, open the link in the email we sent you; if that link has stopped working, sign up again in a few days."; "You are signed in with a different email. Use that email, or sign out first."; "You are already part of this coaching space."; "You were on this coaching space's team, so you can't sign up to train in it. Talk to its owner."; the two under "coming back" above; "That was quick. Give it a moment and send again." and "The form needs to be opened again before it can be sent." (Talk to us's own two); "Enter your date of birth." and "Check the year of your date of birth."; and for the admin "Enter a whole number from 0 to 10,000, or leave it empty. Empty or 0 keeps sign-up closed." and "Enter an age from 18 to 100, or leave it empty, which keeps sign-up closed. TB Gym is for adults, so the age cannot be under 18." The coach history of someone who signed up reads "signed up" where an invited client's reads "joined". Recommendation: read them once; each is one line to change.
- **N9a1, the consumer terms on the admin's Legal documents page.** The row is server only, but the admin publishes documents on a screen, so the page lists Consumer terms as a fourth document ("Asked of people who sign up to train with TB Gym, with no invitation."), the publish sheet offers it, accepted "once per person" by default (it is asked in one space only, TB Gym's own), and the page's two sentences about who is asked now name signing up. Nothing is published and no wording exists. The health intake consent is not asked at sign-up, since a trainee's health is asked only on Max (decision 33): N9c5 must ask for it when it opens the Health step. The other way: wait for N9a2 to add the document to the page. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a1, a space marked a second time.** The house's settings are never deleted, so a space that was cleared and marked again still has the figures it was given, and sign-up can be open the moment it is marked; the command says so ("Its settings from an earlier mark still stand and sign-up is open"). Clearing is refused anyway once the house holds a sign-up or a trainee. The other way: start a re-marked space from empty settings. Recommendation: keep; it only arises while setting the house up.
- **N9a1, what the public page can read.** While sign-up is open or full: the house's name, its clock, the age and, when full, the day it opens again; for someone signed in, also whether they are a client of the house already or have a sign-up waiting for a place. While closed: nothing, not even whether a house exists. An email that already has an account is told so, as the coach's sign-up tells it (the accepted trade-off of a registration form).
- **N9a1, found outside the row.** The coach's sign-up asks for its confirmation email after the account is stored, so a rare failure between the two leaves an account with no email on its way and no way to ask for one (the trainee's sign-up now recognises its own repeat and still asks; a coach's has no sweep to free the address either). Recommendation: fix it with "send the link again", above.
- **N9a1, the technical choices.** The house's settings are rows of one Tenancy table, one per change, each a name and a value kept as text, so a later row adds a setting without a migration; they and the sign-ups are tenant-owned rows of the house, which a coach's space can never read, and database triggers keep them, and the self-directed mark, in the house alone. The week's count and the place it allows are taken under one lock, so the last place is given once; a confirmation, a signed-in join and the sweep all lock the sign-up's own row first. The form's stamp has a purpose of its own, so a stamp from Talk to us is refused. Coach attention moved to version 9. The migration reverts cleanly while no space is marked and refuses to once a house, a setting, a sign-up, a trainee or a consumer terms document exists.
- **N9a2, the offer's words.** `/train` reads "Train on your own, with no coach. Pick exercises, log your workouts and keep your history." and "Creating an account is free.", which is Free as N9 describes it (decision 26) once N9b3 and N9b4 are built; until then a trainee who signs up reads only Today's "Your workouts will start here.". Nobody reads either line before the owner fills the house's settings, since sign-up stays closed until then. The other way: a line true today ("An account of your own with TB Gym"). Recommendation: read them once before turning sign-up on, and turn it on after N9b4.
- **N9a2, search engines and `/train`.** The page asks search engines to leave it out while sign-up is closed or its read failed, and says nothing while it is open or full (the row's default); the form, `/train/sign-up`, always asks to be left out. `robots.txt` and nginx are unchanged, since only the server's answer that day says whether sign-up is open. The other way: leave `/train` out until the owner says. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a2, counting `/train`.** Not counted (the row's default): neither page starts the analytics script, and N1's list of counted pages is unchanged. The other way: count `/train` as the coach's sign-up page counts, one word in `COUNTED_PAGES`. Recommendation: count it the day sign-up opens, to compare visits with sign-ups.
- **N9a2, Today before N9b3.** A self-directed trainee's Today reads "No workouts yet" over "Your workouts will start here." (the row's line, with a heading of its own, since "Nothing assigned yet" speaks of a coach) and offers nothing to tap; it waits for the trainee's own profile before drawing that card, so a coach's line never shows first. The Training page's and the player's empty lines ("Your coach will put your program here.", "Your sessions will appear when your coach shares them.") are unchanged: a trainee reaches neither before N9b1, and both are N9b3's screens. Recommendation: N9b3 replaces them with Start a workout.
- **N9a2, the chip on a phone.** From 561 px Today's chip reads "Self-directed" over the space's name; on a phone, as for a coach's client, only its mark shows, and a screen reader hears "Self-directed · TB Gym coaching space". Me names it in full on every screen. The other way: a line under the heading on a phone. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a2, TB Gym's mark for the house.** The house's avatar on Today and Me reads "TB", TB Gym's own mark as the signed-out frame draws it, not the initials of the space's name ("TS" for "TB Gym coaching space"); on Today the house's logo takes its place once its Owner uploads one in Settings › Brand. The card's words come from the space's own name, so renaming the space renames the card. The other way: the name's initials, as for a coach. Recommendation: keep, and upload TB Gym's logo to the house.
- **N9a2, the clock line.** "Training days follow Beirut time." from the house's zone and, for a visitor whose device is on another offset at that moment, "On your device’s clock, a new training day starts at 9:00 PM." (the house's midnight on theirs; it moves with daylight saving). The other way: the city alone. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a2, the age before sending.** The form shows no age line beside the date of birth (that sentence is the lawyer's) and checks no age itself; someone under the house's age reads "Sign-up is open to people aged 19 or older." under their date of birth once the server refuses it. The other way: say the age before sending, with the lawyer's sentence. Recommendation: add it with the lawyer's words before sign-up opens.
- **N9a2, someone confirmed into a full week.** They sign in to the "You are not part of a coaching space right now" screen, unchanged as N9 asks, which tells them to ask a coach for an invitation; only `/train`, signed in, says their sign-up waits for a place and offers "Finish signing up" once one is open. The other way: a line on that screen pointing to `/train` while their sign-up waits. Recommendation: that line, as a small row.
- **N9a2, signing up with an account one has.** Someone signed in, such as a coach's client (decision 34), reads "Sign up with this account" on `/train`, types their names and date of birth (the server asks for them) and lands on Today in TB Gym's space, which the app selects; their coach's space stays in the switcher, and Me lists both under "Your coaches". Recommendation: keep; "Your coaches" is a words follow-up.
- **N9a2, the House page.** `/admin/house`, "House", linked last after Coaches from the admin's account menu, the member bar, Me's admin rows and Platform billing's header. It names the house (a link to its billing page), its clock and first day, where sign-up stands this week (the server's status, places taken of the number, sign-ups waiting) and the two settings ("New sign-ups a week", "Youngest age that may sign up") with who changed each and when, each changed in a sheet; while no space is marked it names the command. The other way: a panel on the house's own billing page. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9a2, the demo's house has no trainees.** The demo now builds "TB Gym coaching space" beside either cast, marked as the house by the platform-admin command, with an owner (`nadim@tbgym.example`) and a coach (`joelle@tbgym.example`) who are made up, and every setting empty, so its sign-up is closed and nobody is in it: every way a trainee arrives needs the settings filled, which the demo never writes (CLI-023), and the test copy on Render, which restores the demo, keeps a closed sign-up. The other way: open sign-up during the build, sign a few trainees up, then empty the settings, which leaves the demo's figures in the settings' history. Recommendation: keep; a live pass fills the settings on its own copy, as N9a2's did.
- **N9a2, the photo beside the sign-up.** From 1024 px a visitor's `/train` and its form keep the signed-out frame's photo (a coach beside a lifter) and its line "The work behind every win.", but not the summary written for coaches ("Training, meals, check-ins and messages for every client, in one place."), which names what Free does not include. The other way: a photo of someone training alone and a line of the owner's. Recommendation: a trainee photo when the homepage's pictures are next taken.
- **N9a2, the words the row did not name.** `/train` closed: "Sign-up is closed right now." and "If a coach invited you, open the link in their email instead."; signed in: "Sign up with this account", "You’re already training with TB Gym." with "Open Today", "Your email is confirmed, and a place is open now." with "Finish signing up", and while full "Your email is confirmed and your sign-up is waiting for a place. Come back then to finish it."; the form: "Create your account." for a visitor and "Sign up to train." with "Sign up using <email>." for someone signed in, the confirmation "Check your email", "We sent a link to <email>. Open it to finish signing up." and "The link works for one day. This week’s places go to the people who open their link first.", "Too many sign-ups from this connection for now. Try again in an hour.", and for a session that ended elsewhere "You were signed out, so this form now creates a new account. To sign up with the account you have, sign in again."; the tab titles "Train with TB Gym" and "Sign up | Train with TB Gym"; the House page's "New sign-ups a week", "Youngest age that may sign up", "Places taken", "Waiting" ("Email not confirmed yet, or confirmed into a full week"), "Never set", "Changed by <name> on <day>", and its toasts ("New sign-ups a week is now 0, so sign-up is closed."). The refusals are N9a1's sentences. Recommendation: read them once; each is one line to change.
- **N9a2, found outside the row.** A self-directed trainee still reads "your coach" one tap from Today: the empty Notifications page behind the bell ("When your coach sets up a program, shares a week or leaves a note on a workout, it shows here."), Me › My plan's "Your coach will put your plan here." (until N9b1 gives them Free), and on Me "Emails from your coach and quiet hours" under Notifications and "Your goals, health details and contact info" under My profile, though nobody coaches them and the health step is Max's (decision 33). Recommendation: N9b3 words the inbox and Me for a trainee, N9b1 shows Free on My plan, N9c5 the health step.
- **N9a2, the technical choices.** Both pages are one lazy route of their own (`/train`, children loaded on demand) with their own API service outside `ApiClient`, so the first download carries only the two routes and the four House links: 517.70 kB before, 518.31 kB after, of 520 (510 is the warning). A trainee's Today and Me read their own profile before the coach's name, so a coach's client's Today and Me make one more small request before the coach's chip appears, and a trainee's browser never asks for the staff member their record names. "Open Today" picks TB Gym's space by its name among the person's memberships, since the server's answers name no id. The House page's Change sheet asks before Browser Back drops a typed number, as Legal documents' does.
- **N9b1, Free's length and when it renews.** A Free row is 365 days, a fixed length as the row's default asks, and the Worker adds the next from 30 days before it ends, starting on the day that row ends (the day after its last covered day) and linked to it, so the rows run on with no gap: a Worker down for weeks still renews in time, and a row that ran out meanwhile is renewed from its own end. The house's staff see the rows on the client's page with their dates. The other way: an open-ended enrollment, which touches the half-open periods and platform billing and needs a domain decision. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b1, who reads the pool.** Any current client of the house while Training is open to them, not only a self-directed trainee: Free runs under every tier, so a Max trainee, coached and not self-directed (N9c5), keeps it, and a tester the house invited reads it while their plan grants Training. A coach's workspace has no pool. The other way: self-directed trainees alone. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b1, a client the house invites gets no Free.** Free starts when a sign-up is confirmed or someone joins with the signed-in form (and the sweep starts it for a self-directed trainee who has none); a client the house invites the ordinary way gets none, such as the test's testers (Step 2), and so does someone the house invites back after it ended their coaching, since sign-up is closed to them (CLI-024): the house's staff book their plans by hand as today. The other way: every client of the house gets Free. Recommendation: keep while the test runs; decide when testers move to sign-up.
- **N9b1, My plan for a trainee.** The row is server only, so My plan's screen is unchanged: the read now carries Free apart (`free`: its name, since when, its state) and the tier, and Free is never among `plans` with an end date, so a trainee on Free alone still reads "Your coach will put your plan here." (B10a's empty line, which N9a2 left as it was and which names a coach to someone nobody coaches). The row's default, one line "Free, with no end date", needs a screen. The other way: Free listed as a plan with its last day. Recommendation: N9c2's plans page shows Free's line from `free` and words the empty state for a trainee.
- **N9b1, Free in Plans you sell and on the client's page.** The house's Plans you sell lists Free (`houseTier` Free) with every trainee on it, and the client's page lists each Free row with its dates; every change to them is refused by the server (409 `house_free_plan`), but those screens still offer their buttons. The other way: hide Free from the staff altogether. Recommendation: N9c2 marks Free as TB Gym's own plan and hides its edit, price, availability, renew, pause and cancel controls.
- **N9b1, a plan already called Free.** Plan names are unique in a space (BILL-7), so `house set` refuses a space that already sells a plan called Free and marks nothing until it is renamed; a house marked before N9b1 whose staff sell one lets trainees join without Free (a warning is logged) and the sweep starts it once the plan is renamed. The other way: give the house's plan another name. Recommendation: keep; rename such a plan before marking.
- **N9b1, `house clear`.** It takes the house's Free off sale with the mark (the plan stays, sold to nobody, and the name Free is the space's own again), and is refused while a plan of the house's tiers is booked for anyone; marking the space again puts the same Free back on sale. The other way: delete the unused plan, or refuse while it exists. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b1, a paid plan's tier.** The house's staff name Pro+ or Max when they make a plan in the house (`houseTier` on the new plan; never Free, never outside the house, never on a pack), and a plan's tier never changes, so a plan made without one keeps none and a renamed plan keeps its own. No screen sets it yet. The other way: the tier worked out from a plan's features and price. Recommendation: keep; N9c2's plan sheet asks for it.
- **N9b1, where the tier is read.** `GET /api/client-plan/me` carries `tier`: the highest of Max, Pro+ and Free among the house's plans covering today, active and paid in full; none outside the house, while blocked, or with no plan of a tier granting today. When Free and another plan both open Training, the access decision names the other plan. `GET /api/client-access/me` still carries Free's last day when Free alone opens Training, and no trainee's screen shows it today. Recommendation: keep; the screens of N9b3 and N9c2 never show an end for Free.
- **N9b1, the house's Free and the counts.** Every trainee on Free counts as a billable client on the house's platform invoice (PBL-002), among the active clients of Coach Today's This month, in "clients started" (and "ended" when they leave) on the This month page, and as "now" on Free in Plans you sell; nothing excludes them. The test's full discount settles the bill. The other way: leave the house's Free out of billing and of the numbers, a money rule that is the owner's. Recommendation: keep, with the discount, until the owner decides whether the house is billed.
- **N9b1, a program for a trainee.** A program's coverage refuses a Free row ("Free covers a trainee's own workouts, never a program. Choose another plan."), so the house's Coach gives a program only under another plan covering Training: Pro+ or Max once they are sold, until then any plan the staff make. Until N9c3, though, a program's sessions ask only whether Training is open, and Free keeps it open, so a program given under a plan still waiting for its money, paused or cancelled plays on Free's Training. N9c3 builds "a program's sessions open only while the plan it was started under grants". The other way: bring that check forward now, which changes it for every coach's client too. Recommendation: until N9c3, give a trainee a program only once its plan is paid; N9c4 also leaves Free out of Assign program's plans.
- **N9b1, found outside the row: Start a plan's overlap line.** On a trainee's Service page, the web's Start a plan sheet reads Free's Training as blocking and tells the coach "<name> already has Free from … to …. Start on … or later.", a year out, though the server books a plan beside Free today and Save is not blocked. Recommendation: N9c2 leaves Free out of that line (`houseTier` on the web's enrollment model).
- **N9b1, found outside the row: the client page's header.** The web picks a client's current plan itself (the first active plan covering today), so a trainee's page header and Service page can name Free beside a paid plan, though the server's Clients row and page reads name the paid plan. Recommendation: N9c2 lets Free stand for the plan there only when nothing else runs.
- **N9b1, found outside the row: log event ids shared elsewhere.** Ids 5502, 5503 and 5520 (media) and 6201 to 6205 (live messaging and platform billing) each name two different log events, so an alert or a count by id mixes them; N9b1's first build had made 6407 one of the house's own, fixed, and a test now keeps the house's ids 6400 to 6499 one an event. Recommendation: a small row giving each area a range of its own and widening the test.
- **N9b1, the technical choices.** The tier is copied onto each enrollment so the database can keep a trainee's Free rows from overlapping (an exclusion on rows that are Free and not cancelled; the GiST exclusion on coverage is untouched); a start's and a renewal's command ids are derived from the profile and from the row renewed, so a second start or renewal finds the first; the renewal runs on the Worker's hourly house tick beside the sign-up sweep, whose switch (`TraineeSignUps:SweepEnabled`) stops the sign-up removal alone; ending a relationship takes the profile's row lock before it reads the plans, as the renewal does, so a renewal is never left running for someone who left; the pool's search is the coach library's own code. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, who starts a workout of their own.** Any current client of the house while Training is open to them, the pool's own readers (N9b1's line): a self-directed trainee, a Max trainee later, and a tester the house invited while their plan grants Training. A coach's client never: under a coach's header the route answers 404. The other way: self-directed trainees alone. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, its name.** Every workout of one's own is stored as "Workout", where a program's workout keeps its session's name, so history, the Clients row's last activity and the client page read "Workout"; nothing asks the trainee for a name. The other way: a name the trainee types, or one by the time of day ("Morning workout"). Recommendation: keep the stored word; N9b3 may show its own title from `isOwnWorkout`.
- **N9b2, starting from a past workout.** From any finished workout in the trainee's history, a program's included: its exercises still in the pool, in their order, each named as the pool names it now and with as many empty sets as were done there (at least one); an exercise that left the pool is left out, and when none is left the start is refused ("None of that workout's exercises is in the exercise pool now."; a workout finished with no exercise answers "That workout has no exercises to start from."). Nothing done there is carried in. Beside each set stands last time (TRN-029), the lift's latest earlier day from whichever workout, so that workout's numbers show only when it is that day, and never when it was finished the same day. The other way: the exercises alone, with no sets, a refusal as soon as one has left the pool, or the source's own numbers sent beside each set. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, a second start.** While one is unfinished, a start is refused (409 `own_workout_in_progress`, "You have a workout you have not finished. Carry on with it, or finish it first."), a double tap included, and Today's read names the unfinished one. The other way: answer with the unfinished one. Recommendation: keep; N9b3's Start becomes "Carry on" while one is unfinished.
- **N9b2, finishing with nothing logged.** Allowed, as for a program's workout, so a workout started by mistake is ended by finishing it: it then stays in history with no sets and counts in the week's `ownCompleted`. No Discard exists, since a workout row is never deleted. The other way: refuse a finish with nothing logged and add Discard, which deletes a workout that holds nothing (a new kind of delete). Recommendation: N9b3 asks before finishing with nothing logged; Discard only if testers start by mistake often, the owner's call.
- **N9b2, taking a set away.** Only an exercise's last set, so the sets stay numbered 1, 2, 3 and line up with last time's (TRN-029); a logged set by mistake is corrected by saving it again or unticking it. The other way: any set, leaving a gap in the numbers. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, an exercise with a note.** An exercise the trainee wrote a note on stays in the workout (409 `own_exercise_has_note`, "This exercise has a note, so it stays in this workout."), since notes are append-only and hold their exercise. The other way: no notes on an exercise of one's own. Recommendation: keep; N9b3 says why on screen.
- **N9b2, the week's counts.** Each day of the week's read gains `ownCompleted`, the workouts of one's own finished that were started that day; `scheduled` and `completed` stay the program's sessions, so a trainee's Today still reads its week as before until N9b3 shows the new count. The other way: count them in `scheduled` and `completed` too. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, history's program name.** A workout of one's own is in history with `programName` empty and `isOwnWorkout` true, so the web's history keeps compiling (it maps the name into a text that is never null). The other way: `programName` null. Recommendation: keep until N9b3 reads `isOwnWorkout`.
- **N9b2, what the house's staff see.** A trainee's workouts of their own count in Coach Today's records, the coach's exercise history and records reads, the Clients row's last activity and the client page's last workout, but the coach's workout read answers 404 for one (it shows a program's session), and staff cannot write a note in one (409 `own_workout_staff_note`, "Only the client writes notes on a workout of their own."). The other way: a staff view of a trainee's own workouts. Recommendation: a later row if the partners want to read trainees' logs, after N9d's privacy split.
- **N9b2, notes and notices.** A note the client writes on a workout of their own is kept and tells nobody, whoever their coach is (a Max trainee too), since nothing links a notice to a workout with no program. The other way: a notice to the coach for a coached client of the house, which needs a notice that names no program. Recommendation: keep until Max (N9c5) decides what a Max trainee's own workouts tell the coach.
- **N9b2, "Last time + step".** A workout of one's own is never read by where a lift stands (STR-019): it asked no reps, and nothing on Free is worked out. So a Pro+ or Max trainee's own workout of a lift their program steps does not move the step. The other way: count it, as "from any program" reads. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, the demos.** An exercise of a workout of one's own lists the pool's demos as they are now, not a copy made when it was added, so an exercise the house archives later shows no demo in history. The other way: a copy of the demos, which are append-only rows and would keep an exercise from being taken away. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, the limits and closing.** At most 40 exercises and 20 sets an exercise (a program session's limits, the row's default); an unfinished one never closes by itself and stays Today's "Carry on" (the row's default), and it stays unfinished on the record of a trainee who leaves. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, ten a day.** A client starts at most ten workouts of their own on one of the house's days (409 `own_workout_daily_limit`, "You have started 10 workouts today, the most one day holds. Start the next one tomorrow."), a constant beside the limits above: a workout is never deleted, and starting from a past one writes up to 840 rows at once, so this bounds what one account can store (found by the review). The other way: a house setting the platform admin fills, or no cap. Recommendation: keep the constant; no real day reaches it.
- **N9b2, `house clear`.** The command now also refuses while the house holds any workout of one's own (an invited tester's included), as it refuses for a plan of the house's tiers since N9b1, so such a workout never ends up in an ordinary coach's space where the pool is gone. The other way: let the mark go and refuse the workout's routes outside the house, which would leave an unfinished one that can never be finished. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b2, the technical choices.** The same workout tables, the program, session and prescription ids empty together and only together (database checks, and the exercise and set triggers refuse an insert whose shape differs from its parent's); a partial unique index keeps one unfinished; the triggers let only an unfinished workout of one's own lose an exercise or its last set (a set also when its exercise goes in the same delete), never a workout row or any row of a program's workout; a revert of the migration is refused while any workout of one's own exists (roll forward); `HousePool` is the pool's one definition, shared with the pool's read. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, the picker's order.** By name, as the pool's read sorts it (the first half of the row's default). "The trainee's most used first" is not built: no client read says how often a trainee used an exercise (history items name none), so it needs a server read, which this web-only row does not add. The other way: by muscle. Recommendation: a "your exercises first" read with N9b4's pool page.
- **N9b3, an added exercise's first set.** An exercise picked from the pool comes with one empty set to type into (the screen asks for the exercise, then for a set, since the server adds none); "Add set" adds more. The other way: no set until Add set is pressed. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, the picker stays open.** After a pick the sheet stays open, the exercise marked "Added" ("Added ×2" the second time), for the next one; Done closes it and the player shows the last one added. An exercise already in the workout is marked "In this workout" and can be added again. The other way: the sheet closes on the first pick. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, the titles.** The stored name, "Workout", is the title on Today's card, in the player and in history; the player's kicker reads "YOUR OWN WORKOUT", its choice menu "Your own · in progress", and history "Your own workout" where a program's name stands. The other way: a title by the time of day, or one the trainee types (N9b2's line). Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, where a set starts.** A set of one's own starts at last time's numbers on the same set, as TRN-029 starts what nothing prescribed leaves open, so one tap repeats last time, and last time stays beside it. The other way: empty boxes with last time beside them. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, kg or lb.** The choice starts where last time's loads put it, else on neither (TRN-016), so a first workout asks for kg or lb before a load is logged; once known it holds for every set of that workout as a chosen one does, so a lift last done in the other unit starts with no load, its last time still beside it (the review found a set labelled kg that would have sent pounds). The other way: a unit kept on the account. Recommendation: keep; an account's unit is a choice for later.
- **N9b3, finishing with nothing logged.** The finish question then reads "Nothing is logged yet. If you finish now, this workout stays in your history with no sets, and you can’t change it afterwards." (N9b2's line asked for it); no Discard. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, taking things away.** "Remove last set" and "Remove exercise" ask first only when something logged would go with them; an exercise a note names shows "This exercise has a note, so it stays in this workout." where Remove would be. The other way: always ask. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, Start from this workout.** On every finished workout in a self-directed trainee's history, a program's included (the server starts from any); while one is unfinished the start is refused and the line under it offers "Carry on". The other way: only on workouts of one's own. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, the empty pages' words (the row's Open).** Today: "Your workout", "Pick exercises from TB Gym’s pool and log each set as you go." and "Start a workout"; the player with nothing in progress: "No workout in progress" and "Start one, add exercises from TB Gym’s pool and log each set as you go."; an empty workout: "No exercises yet" and "Add your first exercise from TB Gym’s pool, then log its sets."; Training: "Train on your own" and "Start a workout, pick exercises from TB Gym’s pool and log each set. Your finished workouts are below."; when the profile cannot be read, "No workouts yet" and "Your workouts will start here.". Recommendation: read them once; each is one line to change.
- **N9b3, the week strip.** A trainee's week reads "{n} workouts done" (or "No workouts yet") from the workouts of one's own finished, a day with one is drawn done, and an empty day says "No workout" to a screen reader; a client with a program and workouts of their own reads "x of y sessions done · n of your own". The other way: count them in the sessions. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, who is offered Start.** A self-directed trainee alone (the profile's mark, as Today's chip reads it); a tester the house invited or a Max trainee, whom the server lets start one, is offered none, though Today shows Carry on to anyone with one unfinished. The other way: ask the pool's read, which answers 404 outside the house. Recommendation: keep until N9c4 and N9c5 build those trainees' screens.
- **N9b3, the demo in the picker.** Each pool exercise's demo opens with the coach's demo button, in a sheet of the person's own mode over the dark player (as the player's route says of its demo), at a phone's touch size; a YouTube or Vimeo link opens on its site. N9b4 brings the looping clip. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b3, share cards.** As for any workout: the finish summary's record cards, whose byline reads "<first name> · coached by TB Gym coaching space" for a trainee, and the share sheet says "your coach never posts for you". The other way: a trainee's own byline ("trains with TB Gym"). Recommendation: a words follow-up with the share card.
- **N9b3, found outside the row: "your coach" one tap from Today.** Unchanged since N9a2's line: the empty Notifications page and Me's notification and profile lines still speak of a coach to a trainee. Recommendation: a small words row.
- **N9b3, found outside the row: closed Training in a trainee's words.** The picker says why Training is closed as the house would ("TB Gym has paused your access.", "You no longer train with TB Gym, so training is closed."), but the player's and Training's own closed states still read the coaching words ("Your coach has paused your access.", "Your coaching plan has ended. Contact your coach."), which a trainee reaches only once their access has closed. Recommendation: the house's words there too, in the small words row above.
- **N9b3, found outside the row: Progress.** Progress's training bar counts a program's sessions alone (TRN-033 keeps workouts of one's own out of planned sessions), so a Free trainee reads zeros there until N9b5 counts finished workouts. Recommendation: N9b5, as planned.
- **N9b3, the technical choices.** The player plays a workout of one's own as its own workout shape with nothing prescribed, first in the day, so logging, last time, records, the finish summary and the leave question run unchanged; each change waits for the sets still saving to it and puts the whole workout the server answers in place; one that leaves Today's read (finished elsewhere) stays, read only. The calls live in two small services outside `ApiClient`, so the first download is 518.31 kB before and after; the picker reuses the coach library's paged search, and the demo button gained a touch size. The row's e2e is in `workout-progress.e2e.ts` and `training-program.e2e.ts`, the trainee's player and Training, since `client-training.e2e.ts` is the coach's page (the mix-up N6b settled). Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b4, where the pool is reached.** `/training/pool`, under the Training tab, from "Browse exercises" on Training's "Train on your own" and on Today's start card, for a self-directed trainee alone (the mark that offers Start); the server reads it to any current client of the house. No tab of its own and no link in Me. The other way: a tab for trainees, which would sit in the first download. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b4, an exercise's page.** A page of its own at `/training/pool/{exercise}` (it can be kept, Back works, and its "Exercises" link goes back to the list with its search and filters), not a sheet over the list. The other way: a sheet, which keeps the list's scroll. Recommendation: keep.
- **N9b4, the filters (the row's Open).** Muscle and Equipment, as the coach's library's chips, kept in the address; Muscle finds a main or a secondary muscle, as the server's search does, and the equipment None reads "No equipment". The kit's chip gained a 44 px touch size for a client's thumb. Movement and type are not offered. Recommendation: keep; each more chip is a line.
- **N9b4, which demo plays.** The first uploaded video in the house's order, else the first picture, else a YouTube or Vimeo link; an exercise with more demos offers "See all {n} demos", the demo sheet with each. A workout's exercise names its demos without their kinds, so the player shows the first uploaded video, else picture, and leaves a link to "Watch demo". The other way: the house picks the demo that plays. Recommendation: keep; the loader (N9b6) can put the library's clip first.
- **N9b4, a card with no demo.** It shows the exercise's letters on a quiet tile where a clip would be, as the coach's library draws them, so every card keeps one shape; a link's card shows a play tile and asks nothing until its page is open. The other way: shorter cards with no tile. Recommendation: keep while the pool fills; the library's clips replace the letters.
